- **humanizing-text**: Removes signs of AI-generated writing from text to make it sound natural and human-written. Use this skill when the user wants to humanize text, says something sounds too AI, sounds like AI, asks to make it natural, remove AI patterns, or rewrite naturally. Based on Wikipedia's comprehensive "Signs of AI writing" guide. Detects and fixes 25 patterns including inflated symbolism, promotional language, superficial -ing analyses, vague attributions, em dash overuse, rule of three, AI vocabulary words, negative parallelisms, excessive conjunctive phrases, sycophantic tone, and more. - **hunk-review-viz**: A local page for reviewing commits one at a time, file by file and hunk by hunk, in the order the agent says reads best. The reviewer marks each hunk ✓ approve, ✎ change this or ? question, by a speakable ID ("amber falcon"), and the marks survive rewrites and force-pushes. Use when the user wants to review a branch, PR or commit range commit by commit, says "hunk review", "review viz" or "let me review the commits", or after pr-shape reshapes a branch they will read. Needs the viz skill. ([RascalTwo/ai-setup](https://github.com/RascalTwo/ai-setup/tree/HEAD/skills/hunk-review-viz) · ★2)
- **hunk-reviewer**: Lightweight per-hunk code review dispatched during the hunk-by-hunk commit workflow. Reviews a single diff hunk for bugs, security, style, naming, performance, and test gaps. Returns a structured summary. This skill is invoked automatically by the code change workflow; do not invoke it manually. ([alistardust/dotfiles](https://github.com/alistardust/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/hunk-reviewer) · ★1)
- **hunk-session**: Drive a live Hunk terminal diff review via `hunk session *`. Use when the user has a Hunk session running or wants to review diffs interactively. ([to4iki/skills](https://github.com/to4iki/skills/tree/HEAD/engineering/hunk-session))
- **hunk-staging**: Use when a commit must include only selected hunks or lines from a changed file, including commits that mix partial files with whole files, or when unstaging part of the index. Do not use for ordinary whole-file or directory commits. ([rcdailey/dotfiles](https://github.com/rcdailey/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/home/dot_config/opencode/exact_skills/hunk-staging) · ★1)
- **hunk-watch**: Toggle the Hunk flush watcher on or off for this worktree ([jasonkuhrt/dotfiles](https://github.com/jasonkuhrt/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/home/.claude/skills-library/hunk-watch) · ★1)
- **hunkboard-comments**: Act on the review comments the user left on a hunkboard — fix the code or answer each thread, record the outcome, and republish. Use when the user says they reviewed or commented on the board, pastes text copied from it, or says "check hunkboard". ([bochengyang/skills](https://github.com/bochengyang/skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/hunkboard/skills/hunkboard-comments))
- **hunkboard-publish**: Show the current branch's changes as a pull-request-style review page the user opens in a browser, on this machine or on a phone. Use when the user wants to see or review the diff outside the editor, or says "hunkboard" or "publish the board". ([bochengyang/skills](https://github.com/bochengyang/skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/hunkboard/skills/hunkboard-publish))
- **hunkdiff-upgrade**: Complete a hunkdiff dependency bump for hunk-commit — read the release notes, reconcile the declared extension API generation, the README, and the demo GIFs with what the new Hunk actually does. Use when a Renovate PR bumps hunkdiff, when the user asks whether a new Hunk release needs changes here, or after manually changing the hunkdiff version. ([muzomer/hunk-commit](https://github.com/muzomer/hunk-commit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/hunkdiff-upgrade) · ★3)
- **hunkit**: Show Hunkit usage, or launch Hunk for the current repository's working tree or one specified commit in an existing idle tmux pane directly right of Pi, optionally use an explicit task file for intent context, then explain the focused hunk or walk fixed snapshots in narrative order with explanations, optional focused review, and first-parent commit continuation. Command-only skill. In Pi, invoke via /skill:hunkit; /hunkit is also accepted where that alias is exposed. ([Seybo/dots](https://github.com/Seybo/dots/tree/HEAD/.ai/skills-shared/hunkit))
- **hunkpad**: Use Hunkpad for live diff review between a person and a coding agent. Use when asked to open or share a diff viewer, show branch, git-spice stack, unstaged, or latest-turn changes, read or address inline review comments, resolve feedback, check review status, or track one agent turn. Open Hunkpad automatically only when a repository task will change files. Do not open it for research, planning, investigation, code reading, or other read-only tasks. Do not gate use on CMUX_* environment variables. ([delicb/dotfiles](https://github.com/delicb/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/home/dot_agents/skills/hunkpad) · ★1)
- **hunkproof**: Stamps every hunk of a pull request with what the agent ran after writing it and lists the hunks that were never run. Use before opening or reviewing a PR written with Claude Code, when someone asks "what did you actually run", "which changes are unrun", "give me a receipt for this PR", or wants a run report posted on a PR. Reads the Claude Code transcripts already on disk; no LLM call, nothing leaves the machine except, on explicit request, a redacted PR comment. ([JulienAu/hunkproof](https://github.com/JulienAu/hunkproof/tree/HEAD/skill) · ★1)
- **hunkr**: Answer code-review threads coming from the hunkr Neovim plugin. Use when given a path to a hunkr review.json, or when the user says "hunkr", "review threads", or pastes /hunkr &lt;path&gt;. ([mingo023/hunkr.nvim](https://github.com/mingo023/hunkr.nvim/tree/HEAD/skills/hunkr) · ★2)

([elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter](https://github.com/elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter/tree/HEAD/skills/hunt-agent-prompt-extraction) · ★29)
- **hunt-agent-prompt-injection**: Prompt injection in AI agents — direct (chat box is the trust boundary), indirect (payload planted in data the agent later reads: docs, web pages, emails/tickets, RAG chunks, tool output), multimodal (image/audio/file, ASCII/Unicode-tag smuggling), and multi-turn jailbreaks (crescendo, many-shot, skeleton-key). The #1 agent risk. Injection alone is Informational — score every finding by the SINK it reaches. ([elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter](https://github.com/elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter/tree/HEAD/skills/hunt-agent-prompt-injection) · ★29)
- **hunt-agent-race-condition**: TOCTOU / race conditions in AI-agent tools: double-spend, limit bypass, coupon reuse, privilege flip, inventory oversell — any stateful agent where check and commit aren't atomic. Fire operations in parallel (single-packet beats an LLM 'do it simultaneously' prompt) and PROVE the shared state changed. ([elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter](https://github.com/elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter/tree/HEAD/skills/hunt-agent-race-condition) · ★29)
- **hunt-agent-rag**: Attacks on retrieval-augmented agents and their vector stores — knowledge/corpus poisoning, indirect injection via retrieved chunks, cross-tenant/access-control retrieval bypass (IDOR at the embedding layer), ranking manipulation, embedding inversion/membership inference, and citation spoofing. The dominant enterprise pattern. A poisoned doc that's never retrieved, or a 'retrieved' doc the model invented, is not a finding. ([elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter](https://github.com/elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter/tree/HEAD/skills/hunt-agent-rag) · ★29)
- **hunt-agent-resource-abuse**: Unbounded-consumption / denial-of-wallet attacks on agents — forcing expensive token generation, recursive/agent-fan-out loops, unbounded context/retrieval, costly downstream tool calls, and shared-quota exhaustion that degrades service for other tenants. The finding is a MEASURED cost or availability impact with an amplification ratio, not 'it felt slow'. Prove the multiplier and extrapolate; don't actually run up the bill. ([elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter](https://github.com/elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter/tree/HEAD/skills/hunt-agent-resource-abuse) · ★29)
- **hunt-agent-sandbox**: Confinement/boundary hunting for apps that forward user input into a coding-agent subprocess (OpenAI Codex CLI, Claude Code, etc.). Source-first map of the sandbox boundary, then 9 neutral-signal probe classes (read/write escape, procfs env, secret-env, exec-identity, workspace isolation, egress, config-poisoning, tool inventory). Prompts stay out of context — companion probe-prompts.md + deepseek.py gen. ([PhanNhatTien090/claude-redteam](https://github.com/PhanNhatTien090/claude-redteam/tree/HEAD/_global/skills/hunt-agent-sandbox) · ★1)
